THE 6BAIS SUPPLY.
Tb® excitement in Eastern grain cir
cles has been sufficient to advance the
pricQ of wheat in New York some 5 cents
per bushel It was feared the greater part
f ouc stock was destroyed, and that
msineps in this city was 100 much para
ys'jd hy the shock to ndmit of our produce
n*rchan(s taking any further part in the
nrwardinp of grain this autumn- Hence,

tctnnlly dag up the old cry of “bread or
Iriod as one of the possibilities among

•a*e. The grain d *stroyi*d f iota up a total

to a noint where it will not pay to ship

There is no need, therefore, to antic!-
oate even a temporary famine, or even so*

quence of. iiT Chicago. The
Nnrlhwestts full of grain, and the flaw

tbo distributing of supplb-s are expected from

homes bavo been entered in tbe rce
■*rd«. Tbe districting ol tho
city has also been completed, and responsible
tendents appointed In each division.

bution of supplies, pending tbe inauguration of

MEETING OF THE BOARD OF
TRADE.
South Side Headquarters at Standard
Hall.
Tho member* of the Board of Trade who favor
tbo holding of their meeting* 00 the' South Side
assembled at Standard Hall, corner of Thir
teenth street and Michigan avenue, at 10 o'clock
yo*terday morning. There were over U.roo bun
dr* d present. M. G. Liim presided, and George
Counsel acied ss Secretary.
Mr. N. K. Fuirbank offered tbe following reso
lution*. woioh wero adopted:
Wurbrah, A nuccb-r of members of the
Board ot Trade, finding it Inconvenient to at

one year; therotore.
Hctohed, That it is (ho sense of this meeting

members of tbo Board of Trade who dosire tu

pose in any way to sever mir connection with the
R .ard of Trade, but simply meet here for tbe
irmisaetioo of burioet?, until our new ball is
completed, conducting all our business undor the
rules of the Board of Trade, and as members
thereof.
’/fewfrerf, That tho meetings of this board bo

nt tbit the banks of I'hicneo wM
M’V l”*l «'n l d.y. T-ejiUy. Oct. 17
The {result of the conference of the National
lUuk« win* tne Comptroller os the Currency on
night, was ?i encouraging that the
banks unanimously agreed to rescind their res
cind* n* in regard to pay
ing fifteen per cent at once, and further install
meats from time U time during the next few
weeks. It is proper to say here, once for all.
that the resolution to pay by installments be
ginnlngwlih one oflb per cent was adopted solely
os a precautionary measure to prevent tbe pos
sibility of any panic which might begin with de
positors, am! having begun, would inevitably
extend to the whole commercial world. Time
*n-ugh, however, has elapsed since tne resolu
tions referred to were passed, tor tbo hanks to
bate received asiurnnces from great numbers of
their depositors to the effect that they would act
with prud'Qce ami consideration. From the
bank* of New Y»rk, Philadelphia and Boston,
assurances have been received by the banks here
that no reasonable aid will be withheld in case
it is needed. It may seem strange that perfectly
solvent banks.as those o! Chicago unquestionably
arc. should require so much But
u i? wc<l ui dcrsiu d by business men that there
i> not a bank in the United States thathas ready
cash enoucb on baud to pay off all
its depositors ot once. There would
be no profit in banking on such a
basi*. A bank receives inon»-y of one set of
cust<-tn<rs on deposit, and loans these dtp isits
as well ns its own capital to another set of
customers. A demand on the part of all the
depositor* for their money would therefore be
nothing less than a demand that nil
commercial debts should bo paid
at once in cash. whether such
debt* wore due or not. Tho hanks ate merely
the agents of the owners ot capital. 2i these
owners of capital demand tho payment of al)
debts at once, by demanding the payment ot
their deposits, tho di-tress falls not upon tho
banks, but upon th»*,busincss cmiiiunity whom
the banks must press for pa>uiont. Jt was fear
ed that this situation wa* n»«t tuily appreciated
by the public—hence tho resolutions passed Ja*t
Thursday. Now, h-wvver, the aspect ot affairs
is changed* tne public have had time to consider
the muallon. while tuc banks have been getting
iheir money and b-«ks out of their vaults.
3Joocy has been Qownig into the city from a
thousand source?, tho railroads alouc, «ro pour
ing in largo amounts of currency from tbeir cur
rent receipts for fre ght and passengers, and
hundreds of thousands ot dollars arc coming in
to be applied to the relief oi the sufTer.ng. AM
theseamountsmust find a place of deposit, and
were pressed upon the banks even betorc they
had places 10 keep the money. Tu.Jay, banking
LuJiucss is resumed, checks ot depositors will be
paid its usual, and tho largo amounts
of m mey which hare been seeking u place of de-
will find it in the banks. Wo think it
would perhaps have been better it the banks had
dejerred their opening a day or two longer, until
they could have pcrtccled their office arrange
ment 4 aud got their book?, paper?, aud money
iu a U'.tle better order, but tno bankers arc
anxious to d-> everything to meet all reasonable
demands oi their customer?, and notwithstanding
rbe ob3iM*k-s- of inadtquate office rooms, wo may
-ay that business is resumed and confidence is
restored.

A Correction,
There was published in Saturday’* Tribune a
letter reflecting upon the conduct of the Pitts
burgh Sc Fort Wayno Railroad Company in re
fusing, to honor the passes given by the Belief
Committees. It is tho barest justice to that road,
which has done all that is la its power for the
homeless and helplcs*, to stnto that the crowd
ol persons seeking to get Ease over this road
was so great that, for a few days, they were
compelled to discriminate and to send forward
only the women and children and those who
were nnabjc to take care of themselves, refus
ing tor the moment to transport people who were
tblo-bodied and were in no special baste.
Their trains have been crowded to excess, and
they have not had room for paying passengers.
They deserve the praise of all, and the person
who wrote the letter of complaint would not
have done so bad bo understood tho special cir
cumstances of the case.

The fate of Mrs. Frodsb&m, wife ol the well
known art connoisseur, and a sister, we believe,
to Mr. Seguln, the operatic artist, was a sad one.
The family lived in the North Division. Oa the
night of the fire Mr. Frodsbam was away. His
wife, who bad been an invalid for a long time,
was quite ill on that evening, and was moved to
tbe German Luhtcran Church on Sedgwick street,
where, in addition to her sickness, she became
partially insane. Mr. Frodsham spent two days
and nights trying to find her and was at last suc
cessful. She wts then removed to the residence
of William C. Dow. Esq., in the West Division,
whore she died on Sunday.

11. M. Uooloy, the owner of the late Clark
Street Opera House, meets with a total loss. He
bad just retired from business and was about to
go East to live, bavioc rented bis Opera House
;or fire year» at 821,000 per year. He has noth*
icg leit now but the ground, for which bo has
already bad liberal offers. Ho has declined
them, however, preferring to bold on nod build
again.

terday from tbo AJtna Company, of Hartford.

TIIE COURTS.
The several courts of the County hare taken
possession, by the permission of the city, of the
High School building, and the process of dis
tributing rooms .is progressing. New basinesa
will commence with some degree of regularity
to-day. it being understood that the Judges will
attend at 10 a. m.. and at 2 p. m. each day. to
grant remedial orders only.
circuit court.
The October term ol the Circuit Court com
mences by law, and, in laet, yesterday Chief
Justice Williams opened court and announced
that the court would be in daily session. The
Superior Court will reconvene when the lapsed
term shall hare expired, to wit, on the first
Monday of November.
Bia ixsßTiso—rns library.
Amsetingof members of the bar was held
yesterday afternoon in the newly selected Sup
erior Courtroom, at which Judge Porter, of toe
Superior SCourt. presided. The object of the
meeting as stated by Judge Gary, was to devise
legislation necessary to the relief of city and
of Cook County.
As a result of the meeting it was resolved
that toe Judgesof thoconrts of record in Cook
County be requested to take fsueh measures as
they may deem best to secure the temporary use
of the Supreme Court Library at Ottawa.
